Document 002: 1-9月财政收支数据解读：9月财政收入同比转为微降

Source PDF: all report pdf/1-9月财政收支数据解读：9月财政收入同比转为微降.pdf
Report source: 国新证券股份
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

市场研究部 2026年10月25日
事件
10 月 23 日，财政部发布，1-9 月，全国一般公共预算收入
172292亿元，同比增长 5.1%；全国一般公共预算支出 210199亿
元，同比增长 1%。
解读
一、1-9 月财政收入增速比 1-8 月小幅回落，9 月财政收入同
比转为微降。
1-9月，全国一般公共预算收入 172292亿元，同比增长 5.1%，
比 1-8月回落 0.6个百分点，比 2025年同期提高 4.6个百分点。
其中，9 月，全国一般公共预算收入 15659 亿元，同比下降
0.1%，比 8月下滑 4.8 个百分点，比 2025 年同期回落 2.7 个百分
点。
1-9月，全国税收收入同比增长 6.1%，比 1-8月回落 0.5个百
分点，比 2025年同期提高 5.4个百分点；非税收入同比增长 1.2%，
比 1-8月回落 0.3个百分点，比 2025年同期提高 1.6个百分点。
其中，9 月全国税收收入同比增长 0.2%，比 8 月回落 5.4 个
百分点，比 2025 年同期回落 8.5 个百分点；非税收入同比增长

5.7%，比 8 月提高 6.9 个百分点，比 2025 年同期提高 17.1 个百
分点。
分中央和地方看，1-9 月，中央一般公共预算收入同比增长
8.3%，比 1-8月回落 0.8个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入同
比增长 2.7%，比 1-8月回落 0.4个百分点。
9月，中央一般公共预算收入同比增长 0.2%，比 8月回落 9.1
个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入同比-0.34%，比 8 月回落
1.05个百分点。
二、9 月国内增值税、国内消费税同比增速比 8 月提高，企业
所得税、进口税收、个人所得税、证券交易印花税等税种增速比8
月回落，证券交易印花税同比转为下降。
1-9 月，国内增值税 55416 亿元，同比增长 6%，比 1-8 月提
高 0.1 个百分点。其中，9 月同比增长 7.3%， 比 8 月提高 3.3 个
百分点。
宏观研究
9月财政收入同比转为微降
——1-9月财政收支数据解读
市场表现截至 2026.8.31
资料来源：Wind,国新证券整理。
分析师：郝大明

登记编码：S1490514010002

证券研究报告

国内消费税 12402 亿元，同比下降 4.1%，降幅比 1-8 月扩大
0.5个百分点。 其中，9月同比下降 8.5%，降幅比 8月收窄 5.5个
百分点。
企业所得税 34832 亿元，同比增长 7.1%，比 1-8 月回落 0.2
个百分点。 其中，9 月同比增长 0.4%， 比 8 月下滑 12.0 个百分
点。
个人所得税 13464亿元，同比增长 14.1%，比 1-8月回落 0.4
个百分点。 其中，9 月同比增长 10.5%， 比 8 月回落 1.3 个百分
点。
进口货物增值税、消费税 15171 亿元，同比增长 12.7%，比
1-8月提高 0.1 个百分点。其中，9月同比增长 12.9%， 比 8月回
落 2.0个百分点。
出口货物退增值税、消费税 18087亿元，同比增长 6.3%，比
1-8月提高 1.8个百分点。 其中，9月同比增长 29.0%， 比 8月提
高 30.7个百分点。
1-9 月，印花税 4121 亿元，同比增长 31.1%，比 1-8 月回落
4.5个百分点。其中，9月同比下降 11.7%， 比 8月下滑 26.8个百

分点。
1-9月，证券交易印花税 2391亿元，同比增长 65.1%，比 1-8
月回落 16.9 个百分点。 其中，9月同比下降 11.5%， 比 8月下滑
29.4个百分点。
三、1-9 月全国财政支出小幅增长，比 1-8 月小幅回落，9 月
同比微增。
1-9月，全国一般公共预算支出 210199亿元，同比增长 1%，
比 1-8月回落 0.2个百分点。
分中央和地方看，中央一般公共预算本级支出 32820 亿元，
同比增长 5.8%，比 1-8 月回落 0.2 个百分点；地方一般公共预算
支出 177379亿元，同比增长 0.2%，比 1-8月回落 0.1个百分点。
1-9月，财政支出同比增长的项目是，卫生健康支出同比增长
7.7%，比 1-8月回落 1.5个百分点，社会保障支出同比增长 5.2%，
比 1-8月回落 1.3个百分点，债务付息支出同比增长 4.7%，比 1-8
月回落 0.7 个百分点，科学技术支出同比增长 3.0%，比 1-8 月提
高 0.8 个百分点，教育支出同比增长 1.1%，比 1-8 月提高 0.4 个

百分点。
其余支出项目 1-9 月均同比下降，其中，农林水支出同比下降
5.1%，节能环保支出同比下降 12.5%，文化旅游支出同比下降
6.0%，交通运输支出同比下降 4.1%，城乡社区事务支出同比下降
1.5%。

### Page 3

四、1-9 月政府性基金预算收入及国有土地使用权出让收入无
同比大幅下降，9月同比降幅比8 月小幅收窄。
1-9 月，全国政府性基金预算收入 25010 亿元，同比下降
18.6%，降幅比 1-8 月收窄 0.4 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央
政府性基金预算收入 3682 亿元，同比增长 12.4%，比 1-8 月提高
0.2个百分点；地方政府性基金预算本级收入 21328亿元，同比下
降 22.3%，降幅比 1-8月收窄 0.6个百分点，其中，国有土地使用
权出让收入 16087亿元，同比下降 27.9%，降幅比 1-8月收窄 0.7
个百分点。
9月，全国政府性基金预算收入同比-15.9%，降幅比 8月扩大
12.1个百分点；分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金预算收入同比增
长 13.7%，比 8 月回落 40.6 个百分点，地方政府性基金预算本级
收入同比-18.4%，降幅比 8月扩大 8.1个百分点，其中，国有土地
使用权出让收入同比-23.2%，降幅比 8月扩大 10.6个百分点。

1-9 月，全国政府性基金预算支出 51948 亿元，同比下降
17.0%，降幅比 1-8 月扩大 0.6 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央
政府性基金预算本级支出 3175亿元，同比下降 58.3%，降幅比 1-
8月收窄 3.5个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 48773亿元，同
比下降 11.3%，降幅比 1-8 月扩大 1.8 个百分点，其中，国有土地
使用权出让收入相关支出 21786 亿元，同比下降 18.5%，降幅比
1-8月扩大 1.1个百分点。
9月，全国政府性基金预算支出 62754亿元，同比下降 16.2%，
降幅比 8月收窄 5.2个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金
预算本级支出 3692亿元，同比下降 54.4%，降幅比 8月扩大 45.6
个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 59062 亿元，同比下降
11.6%，降幅比 8 月扩大 10.6 个百分点，其中，国有土地使用权
出让收入相关支出 25565 亿元，同比下降 18.5%，降幅比 8 月收
窄 8.3个百分点。

五、预测10月财政收入同比增速小幅提高。
9月国内增值税、国内消费税同比增速比 8月提高，企业所得
税、进口税收、个人所得税、证券交易印花税等税种增速比 8月回
落，证券交易印花税同比转为下降。预测 10 月财政收入同比增速
小幅提高。
投资建议
1、9月国内增值税、国内消费税同比增速比 8月提高。
2、9月证券交易印花税同比转降。
3、9月出口退税同比大幅增加。
4、9月企业所得税、个人所得税同比增速回落。

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補

### Page 3

3

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

［参考文献］ 
浦沢聡士 （2023）「GDPナウキャストと景気判断～景気判断実務におけるGDPナウキャストの活用に向
けて～」 、内閣府経済社会総合研究所「経済分析」第208号 
太田晴康・仲山泰弘・酒井才介・松浦大将・越山祐資・西野洋平（2024） 「 「みずほGDPナウ」の推計
～DFMを用いた日本のGDPナウキャスティング～」 、 みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ 『みずほ
インサイト』 、2024年8月30日 
酒井才介・西野洋平・太田晴康・仲山泰弘（2024）「「みずほ GDP ナウ」で見る景気動向～9 月中旬

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報）予測 
～前期比年率＋1.5％を予想～ 
個人消費は小幅減だった一方、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げ

経済調査部 エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁 
  チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ 11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋
1.5％（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する。個人消費は小幅
に減少したが、設備投資と輸出の増加が実質GDPを押し上げたようだ。 
◼ 個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想す
る。設備投資は 4-6 月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。
公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加したと見込んでいる。 
◼ 輸出と輸入はサービスが減少した一方、財が増加したと予想する。代替調達の進展に
よる鉱物性燃料輸入増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が行
われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質 GDP成長率を押し上げた分が相殺したようだ。

1．2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋1.5％を予想 
11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋1.5％
（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する（10月30日正午までに公表された
基礎統計に基づく）。個人消費は小幅に減少したが、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げたよ
うだ。 
個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想する。設
備投資は4-6月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。 
輸出と輸入はサービスが減少したものの、財が増加したと予想する。中東産原油の代替調達
の進展による鉱物性燃料輸入の増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が
行われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質GDP成長率を押し上げた分で相殺されたようだ。

図表 2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別寄与度の推移

民需：個人消費、住宅投資は減少の一方、設備投資は大幅な増加を予想 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％と予想する。4-6月期のインバウンド（個人消費の控除項目）の実
績が上方改定されるであろうことを踏まえると、2 四半期連続のマイナスとなると見込んでい
る。サービスは増加したものの、耐久財と半耐久財、非耐久財がそろって減少したとみている。
耐久財では、自動車税の環境性能割廃止やエアコンの省エネ基準の引き上げの影響もあって 4-
6 月期に増加していた自動車や家電への支出が減少したようだ。半耐久財・非耐久財では衣服
やたばこ、ガソリンなどが増加したものの、食料品などが減少したとみられる。サービスは外
食や鉄道旅客、娯楽などが増加したようだ。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.8％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7月の建設工事費デフレーター
（住宅総合、大和総研による季節調整値）が前月比＋6.8％と高い伸びを示す中にあっても、7、
8 月平均の新設住宅着工戸数（国土交通省による季節調整値）は、分譲住宅の増加に支えられ
-1.0

### Page 3

3 / 7

4-6月平均比＋1.0％となった（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。しかし、進捗ベースで計上される
GDP統計上は、建設資材等の価格上昇の影響により実質では減少したとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と予想する。建設投資は減少したものの、機械投資と知的財産生
産物投資（ソフトウェア、研究開発など）が増加したようだ。研究開発投資の増加には、4-6
月期に特殊要因で研究開発サービスの受取（設備投資の控除項目）が大幅に増加したことの反
動も含まれる。経済産業省によると、資本財総供給指数は、国産品と輸入品が共に増加し、7-
9 月期は前期比＋4.9％となった（巻末の関連指標②中段左）。一方、建設投資は、国土交通省
によると、民間非居住用建築・土木の出来高（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）の7、8月
平均は 4-6月平均比＋1.2％となったが、7月の建設工事費デフレーター（非住宅総合、大和総
研による季節調整値）は前月比＋7.3％と大きく上昇しており、実質では減少したとみられる。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.0％ptと予想する。仮置き値の原材料在庫は同＋0.1％pt、
仕掛品在庫は同▲0.1％pt となった。その他では製品在庫が同＋ 0.1％pt、流通在庫が同▲
0.1％ptだったとみられる。

公需：公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加と予想 
公共投資は前期比▲1.0％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7、8 月平均の公共工事出来高
（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）は 4-6月平均比▲2.2％と減少傾向にある（巻末の関連
指標②中段右）。25年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一服したとみられる。加え
て、工事費の上昇も重しとなっている。 
政府消費は前期比＋0.2％と予想する。退職金支払いの増加や私立高校授業料の実質無償化、
公立小学校の給食費無償化もあって高い伸びとなった 4-6月期に続き、医療費の増加により 7-
9月期の政府消費も増加したとみられる。 
公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.3％ptと予想する。4-6月期と異なり、7-9月期には大規模
な石油の国家備蓄放出は行われず、公的在庫変動は実質GDP成長率を押し上げたとみられる。

外需：輸入増が輸出増を上回り、外需寄与度は▲0.2％ptを予想 
輸出は前期比＋0.5％と予想する。サービスは減少したものの、財は増加したと見込んでい
る。財では半導体等製造装置や化学製品、鉱物性燃料などが増加したようだ。サービスでは、
4-6月期に大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスの反動減に加え、産業財産権等使用料やイン
バウンドなども減少したとみている。 
輸入は前期比＋1.8％と予想する。輸出と同様、サービスは減少したものの、財は増加した
と見込んでいる。財では、代替調達の進展により鉱物性燃料が大きく増加したほか、電算機類
も増加したようだ。サービスではアウトバウンドは増加したものの、研究開発サービスや著作
権等使用料などが減少したとみられる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

輸入増が輸出増を上回り、純輸出（外需）の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比▲0.2％
ptになったと予想する。

2．10-12月期の実質 GDPはおおむね横ばいを予想 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比でおおむね横ばいになると見込んでいる。個人
消費は小幅に増加、設備投資は増加すると予想する。輸出はAI関連需要を背景とした米国向け
輸出を中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需はマイナスになるとみている。 
個人消費は小幅に増加すると予想する。厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」の現金給与総額は
前年同月比で 3％を超える伸びが続いていることに加え、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上
げられることから、名目賃金の高い伸びは続くだろう。一方、9月の消費者物価指数（除く生
鮮食品）は前年同月比＋2.1％と、8月から 0.4％pt伸び幅が拡大した。政策要因による影響を
差し引いたとしても、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっており、実質賃金上昇率を押し下
げるだろう。エネルギー価格についても、電気・ガス料金補助は12月使用分（1月請求分）か
ら再開されるものの、現行の補助は 9月使用分（10月請求分）で一旦終了するため、10-12月
期の物価は押し上げられる。加えて、ガソリン補助金の基準価格が12月以降引き上げられるこ
とや、燃料費・原料費調整制度によって資源価格高騰の影響がラグを持って電気・ガス料金に
反映されることも物価の押し上げ要因となろう1。 
設備投資は増加すると予想する。日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）によ
ると、9月調査時点での 2026年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、除く土地、含むソフトウェ
ア・研究開発）は前年度比＋9.3％と、前年の 9月調査時点（同＋9.5％）と同程度の伸びが示
された。建設資材価格と労務費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投
資といった設備投資の増加基調は続くと見込む。 
住宅投資は建設資材と労務費上昇の影響を受けて減少すると見込んでいる。公共投資は工事
費の高騰を背景に横ばい圏で推移すると予想する。政府消費は高齢化などに伴う医療費・介護
費増により増加すると見込んでいる。 
輸出は増加すると予想する。米国の設備投資は中東情勢をめぐる不透明感や金利上昇の中に
あっても、AI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。米国内での設備投資の増加は米国向け輸出
の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日
本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、小幅な増加にとどまるだろう。 
輸入は増加すると予想する。経済産業省によると、9月の原油調達について、バブ・エル・
マンデブ海峡経由で調達予定であった原油の輸送ルートをスエズ運河経由に切り替えたことで

1 12月以降の電気・ガス料金補助とガソリン補助金に関する政府の対応については、首相官邸「中東情勢を踏
まえた『緊急的激変緩和措置』の今後の対応についての会見」（2026年10月 29日）を参照。

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

到着が遅れた分は備蓄原油の既放出分で対応したという2。10月は前年平月比 10割の調達が実
現できる見通しとのことであり、11月以降も同量を確保できるのであれば、財輸入は押し上げ
られるだろう。 
リスク要因は中東情勢の悪化とAI関連需要の下振れである。中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格が
さらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押しするだろう。また、足元の米国経
済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などのIT関連投資
がけん引役となっている。10月半ばにかけて、一部AI関連企業の財務上の脆弱性が意識され、
株価が下落する局面があったが、こうした懸念がAI関連企業全体に広がれば、外部資金への依
存度が高い企業を中心に設備投資が鈍化し得る3。その場合、米国向け輸出が下押しされる可能
性があり、注意が必要だ。

2 詳細は、第 5回中東情勢に伴う重要物資の安定的な供給確保のためのタスクフォース（2026年 10月2日）
における経済産業省提出資料を参照。 
3 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年
10月22日）を参照。

### Page 1

ab 27 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Faraday Technology
New opportunities in advanced nodes
Q326 beat on turnkey strength and non-op gains; Q426 outlook positive
Q326 EPS of NT$1.07 came in above UBS/Street expectations mainly on  non-op gains.
Q326 sales came in at NT$3.66 bn (+11% QoQ), ahead of its original guidance but in
line with its comment  during Taiwan Summit with turnkey sales up 20% QoQ given
better support from OSAT and foundries, despite NRE declining 31% QoQ. Q326 GM

decreased 2.7ppt QoQ to 42.5% on higher turnkey mix, while  OPEX grew 9% QoQ  on
salary adjustment and higher bonus accrual. Non-op gain of NT$266mn mainly includes
valuation gains on an investee company. For Q426F, it guided sales  flat  QoQ remaining
at high levels with IP and NRE up QoQ, and turnkey down QoQ. GM is guided back to
~45%, reflecting normalized ASIC economics; OPEX should decline single digit QoQ.
2027 growth outlook supported by AI, advanced packaging and 12nm node

Management guided for double-digit sales growth in 2027, with all three businesses, IP ,
NRE and turnkey, expected to grow, driven by three key catalysts: AI, advanced
packaging and new UMC 12nm project contribution. For IP , growth should be
supported by higher royalties on foundry price hikes/customer capacity expansion and
initial  contribution from 12nm node; NRE should benefit from 2nm/4nm projects
entering peak recognition, with 3-4 ASIC tape-outs targeted in H127; while MP should

see growth in both MCU and ASIC as new projects gradually ramp. AI-related mix was at
8-9% of Q326 sales, and is expected to increase throughout 2027 alongside
accelerating advanced-packaging shipments. We expect 2027E sales to grow 37% YoY.
2nm design win opens new advanced-node and product opportunities
Faraday secured a 2nm GAA special purpose  ASIC project worth several tens of millions
of US dollars in NRE, which management described as its largest-ever design win and

most advanced project to date. The project spans front-end design and IP integration,
back-end design, customized 3D DRAM and advanced packaging, and marks Faraday's
first customized 3D DRAM and hybrid-bonding project. It expects to recognize its first
NRE milestone  in H127, followed by MP targeted within 1-2 years. Beyond 2nm, Faraday
is expanding into new applications including OCS, CXL and Si-cap, leveraging its
capabilities in advanced packaging, interposer design and ASIC integration. We believe

successful execution by Faraday and its foundry partner could strengthen Faraday's
positioning in advanced-node projects and create follow-on design-win opportunities.
Valuation: Raise PT to NT$200, stay Neutral
We raise    2026E EPS (reported) from NT$3.43 to NT$3.68 on better Q326, but lower
2027/28E EPS by 4%/1% to factor in softer CE demand and higher OPEX. We raise our
PT to NT$200 from NT$190 using 24x 2027E PE (vs prior 22x). We raise our multiple as

the recent 2nm project win should boost investor confidence, but stay Neutral as the
advanced nodes (4nm/2nm) turnkey revenue recognition depends on the foundry
partner's execution. We await clearer evidence before revisiting our assumptions.

Company Description
Faraday was founded in 1993, spun off from UMC's IP and semiconductor design service
department as the first IC design service company in Asia. It is an IC design and production
service provider that initially focused on specialty applications on mature nodes by partnering

with UMC and gradually upgraded to advanced nodes through working with Samsung. The
company had its IPO on the Taiwan Securities Exchange in 1999.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Faraday using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced nodes migration, further delay of
advanced packaging ramp, and growing competition in specialty projects from China IC
design service companies.

by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Update
Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Read-across from Celestica's
3Q26 Results
We see positive implications for Accton given demand strength
(1.6T migration amid sustaining 800G) and breadth (scale up
opportunities) of ethernet  switches in future.
Celestica (CLS.N, not covered) 3Q26 results:
• 3Q26 revenue of US$5,830mn (+24% QoQ/+83% YoY), non-GAAP operating
margin of 8.5%,  a record high, and non-GAAP EPS of US$3.30 (+30% QoQ/

+109% YoY).
° Higher than guidance and 7%/13% above consensus for revenue/earnings,
respectively.
° Drivers: Stronger CCS revenue (84% of the mix in 3Q26), including
networking and compute/storage growth of 68% and 242% YoY,
respectively, vs. guidance 60% and 190% YoY.
• 4Q26 revenue guided at US$6,275-6,575mn (+10% QoQ/+76% YoY at the
midpoint) vs. consensus at US$6,396mn, management raising 2026 target to
US$21bn for revenue (+69% YoY vs. prior US$20.5bn) and US$11.55 for non-

GAAP EPS  (+91% YoY vs. prior US$11.3).
° Driven by hyperscaler-led 800G demand and ramping 1.6T programs,
along with continued AI compute and storage demand.
• 2027 revenue guided at US$40bn (+90% YoY), non-GAAP OPM at 9.0%
(+0.6ppt YoY) and non-GAAP EPS at US$21.5 (+86% YoY)
° It expects CCS to grow 110% (1.6T/800G on networking and ASIC ramp
on compute/storage) and  ATS at 10%, with revenue skewed to  2H on
multiple large ramps and momentum likely sustaining into  2028.

• Pluggable should remain the mainstream interconnect solution, while CPO
might pick up more meaningfully from 3.2T, per company.
• CLS expects ethernet scale up could represent new opportunities in future.
Our view:
• While the customer profiles are different, we still see positive implications
from Celestica's comments for Accton regarding demand strength (1.6T
migration amid sustaining 800G) and breadth (scale up opportunities) of
ethernet  switches.

• We stay constructive on Accton, and believe   it will continue to benefit from
the secular growth in network switches amid increasing data traffic, spec
upgrades, and a wider customer base as well as the AI accelerator modules
on the initiatives for compute diversity.

More Takeaways
Further comments from Celestica:
• Demand visibility is lengthening and it is now having conversations with customers
for 2029/2030 projects
• Still seeing lots of pluggables during 1.6T, while it expects CPO to play out more
prominently in 3.2T.
• It has won a new hyperscaler program to co-design and manufacture a 1.6T rack-
scale CPO networking solution, with production scheduled to ramp in late 2027,

per management.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating profit CAGR of 32%.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

목표주가 (하향) 95,000 원
현재주가 56,700 원
상승여력 67.5 %

컨센서스 대비 
상회 부합 하회

VIETNAM 
HAS 
CONTINUE
D 
TO ENJOY VERY STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH
, 
DESPITE EXTERNAL SHOCKS 
Vietnam’s economic success story has continued
 , 
despite last year’s US tariff shock and this year’s energy shock  
◼ 
 Economic growth accelerated to 8% in 2025 and 8.2% y/y in H1 2026, driven by exports, investment and private consumption. 
 It is 
projected to slow in the short term, reaching a still strong 
 level of 
 7.3% in 2026. 
 External accounts strengthened in 
 2025
, but 
they 
have 
 deteriorat
 ed 
in recent months. Public and external debt ratios 
 remain  
moderate.  
◼ 
 Vietnam is highly exposed to the global energy shock
 , 
but 
the consequences have been 
 less severe  
than expected  
so far
 . Economic 
activity 
 has continued to grow 
 strong
 ly
, notably because the export
 -
 oriented manufacturing sector 
 has been 
 boosted by global 
demand for AI goods. 
 F
iscal and current account balances can absorb a deterioration
 . 
 I
nflation has increased 
 while 
 currency 
depreciation pressures  
have remained 
 manageable. 
 Yet, 
as 
 the negative impact of high energy prices 
 is expected to persist, 
 V
ietnam  
could face 
 more 
severe external  
liquidity tensions going forward.

Vietnam’s export base has strengthened further over the past year, notwithstanding the risin
 g 
US tariffs
 , notably 
because 
 Vietnam 
 has been a  
key beneficiar
 y 
of the surgin
 g 
global demand for AI
 -
 related goods  
◼ 
 Export growth strengthened in 2025 (+17% in value), driven by electronic goods (+48%) and sales to the U
 nited 
S
tates 
(+28%). V
ietnam 
was 
 one of 
the main targets of President Trump’s tariff war in early 2025, but the tariff shock was much smaller than initially feared, 
and exports were boosted by surgin
 g 
AI
-
 related demand. V
 ietnam  
increased its global market share (1.9% of world exports 
 in 2025 
vs. 1.7% in 2024), supported by gains in the US market (5.7% of US imports vs. 4.2% in 2024) and the semiconductor sector (
 Vietnam 
is estimated  
to 
account for  
2.9% of world exports of semiconductors vs. 1.6% in 2024).  
◼ 
 Solid growth dynamics in industrial 
 out
p
ut 
and exports ha
 ve 
continued 
 in 2026
 . Exports rose by 
 21.9% y/y in the first eight months 
of the year
 , 
led by the strong increase in both volume and price of tech goods.  
However, 
 Vietnam  
adds little value to the tech goods  
that 
it exports as it speciali
 s
es mostly in assembl
 ing 
and packaging chips to form electronic components. 
 Therefore, i
 ts balance of 
trade of semiconductor goods is negative
 , as i
 mports and exports of tech goods are increasing in tandem.  
◼ 
 V
ietnam
 ’s trade surplus with the U
 nited 
 S
tates 
has 
 surged in the past three years and 
 recently  
bec
a
me the largest bilateral surplus, 
ahead of Mexico and China
 . 
It 
 reach
 ed 
 USD2
 19 
 bn in the 12
 -
month period to 
 July 
 2026
, up 
from USD116 bn in 2022  
(
vs. 
 Mexico’s 
trade surplus with the 
 US: 
USD213 bn  
and China’s trade surplus with the US: 
 USD165 bn  
during  
the same period
 ).  
◼ 
 Meanwhile, V
 ietnam
 ’s imports of Chinese goods have increased rapidly (+3
 6.9
% y/y in 
 the first eight months of 
 2026), causing its 
trade deficit with China to surge.  
Indeed, V
 ietnam  
has played a growing role in the rerouting of Chinese goods to the US. It has also 
benefited largely from the reorgani
 s
ation of production chains in the region and continued to attract 
 significant  
 FDI, mostly from 
Asian countries. Net FDI inflows have exceeded 4% of GDP in recent years, which has fostered continued expansion of the 
manufacturing sector and progress up the value chain.  
◼ 
 In the 
 short term
 , 
solid export growth dynamics are likely to continue, assuming 
 that 
 there is no correction in the AI cycle and 
 that 
manufacturing production 
 is 
 not hindered by energy supply difficulties.  
◼ 
 US trade policy remains 
 a source of 
 uncertain
 ty
. The US effective tariff rate on V
 ietnam’s  
goods, which rose from 3.9% at end
 -
 2024 
to 19.3% in Aug
 ust 
 2025, 
 has fallen 
 back 
 in 2026 (
 as the US Supreme Court cancelled ‘reciprocal’ tariffs)
 ; it is now close to 10
 %. This 
is higher than 
 some South Asian 
 neighbours
 , 
 but not high enough to undermine the competitiveness of 
 Vietnamese 
 goods 
substantially
 . However, Vietnam is 
 still 
 exposed to the risk of new US tariffs, given its connector role between China 
 and 
 the U
 nited 
S
tates 
and its huge trade surplus with the U
 nited 
 S
tates
.

Vietnam  
is a net energy importer highly exposed to the consequences of the conflict in the Middle East. So far, 
the impact of hydrocarbon supply disruptions has been 
 more moderate  
than expected, but it could 
 worsen 
 in 
the coming months.  
◼ 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to disruptions in energy trade flows
 . I
n 2025, 
 88% of its total crude oil imports came from Kuwait and 49% of 
its gas imports  
were 
from the Gulf region. However, a few factors have attenuated the shock  
resulting from the Iran war
 , as  
V
ietnam  
has reacted swiftly, finding alternative suppliers, introducing measures to contain demand and using its (low) stocks. 
 The country 
produces 70% of its needs for refined products and imports the remainder from Asian countries  
(
though the region’s production is 
dependent on imports from the Gulf countries
 )
. More positively, oil 
 and 
gas account for only 35% of 
 Vietnam
 ’s energy mix (coal: 54% 
and 
renewables: 11%), and the country can part
 ial
 ly switch to coal for electricity generation.  
◼ 
 The potential pass
 -
 through of rising global energy prices to domestic inflation is high. Yet, the government has some fiscal policy 
leeway 
 (
given its moderate deficit and debt ratios
 ) 
and has introduced small measures to contain inflation (
 such as the 
 use of 
 the 
price stabili
 s
ation fund  
and 
 fuel
-
 tax suspension until 
 the 
end 
of 
June). CPI inflation 
 rose to 
4.
4
% 
y/y in 
 the first eight months of 
 2026, 
up from 
 3.
3
% in 
 2025
. 
It is projected to 
 average  
4.
4
% in 2026 
 – 
which is still within  
the 
target 
set by the central bank (S
 B
V) 
for this 
year
, 
but also the highest rate since 2013. This will dampen consumers’ purchasing power. The SBV is projected to keep policy rates  
stable in the 
 short term
 , unless inflation accelerates more than expected or depreciation pressures on the dong intensify too much.

VIETNAM HAS NOT YET FIXED ITS EXTERNAL LIQUIDITY PROBLEM
, WHICH IS A SOURCE OF VULNERABILITY 
◼ 
 V
ietnam
 ’s trade balance 
 has 
 deteriorated 
 steadily 
 in 2026. It turned in
 to a deficit  
amounting to 
 USD
19 
bn 
in the first eight months of 
2026 
 (customs data)
 , 
 compared to  
 a surplus 
 in the previous year 
 (
amounting to 
 USD
14 
 bn 
 in the first eight months of 2025
 ).  
This 
 rapid deterioration 
 has resulted notably from the ris
 ing 
import prices of both energy and electronic inputs.  
◼ 
 On the one hand, the current account surplus 
 is 
 decreas
 ing, bu
 t from a comfortable level
 . 
It is projected to narrow 
 to 
2.9
% of GDP in 
2026 from 6.7% in 2025, due to  
the larger energy trade deficit (
 which 
 accounted for  
3.6% of GDP in 2025), the larger trade deficit in 
semiconductors/tech goods, and lower export receipts from tourism 
 (about 
 6% of GDP). 
 Furthermore
 , net FDI  
 inflows will remain 
strong,  
staying close to 4% of GDP in 2026
 . 
Therefore, 
 Vietnam is  
unlikely to need to incur new foreign debt.  
◼ 
 On the other hand, V
 ietnam
 ’s forex reserves are low and do not provide a solid cushion  
 against a deterioration 
 in 
 the balance of 
payments
 . Forex reserves amounted to USD8
 8
.
3 
bn at the end of 
 June 2026
 , which is 
 weak: it is 
 enough 
to service external debt in 
the 
 short ter
 m 
 but covers less than 3 months of imports of goods 
 and 
 services. In 
 particular, i
 f 
 resident capital outflows increase 
(residents’ confidence in the dong 
 may 
be eroded by ri
 s
in
g 
inflation and the external energy shock), currency pressure could intensify 
and the central bank could have difficulties to defend the dong. 
 This risk cannot be ignored
 , 
 even 
 though 
 t
he VND has barely 
depreciated 
 this year (
 the 
central 
 reference 
rate has lost a small 2% against the USD since  
the beginning of 2026
 )
.

MEDIUM
-
TERM PROSPECTS ARE FAVO
U
RABLE 
◼ 
 In the medium term, economic growth is expected to remain strong. It is projected at 5.9% per year on average in 2027
 –
2031, 
compared to  
6.2% in the past decade. This is one of the highest growth rates expected in the ASEAN region.  
◼ 
 This  
projection is based on a rather optimistic scenario, as it assumes that:  
i
) FDI continues to flow in, and the manufacturing export sector expands further
 . This 
 means Vietnam’s external accounts 
 continue 
to 
strengthen in the medium term, thanks to continued 
 current account surpluses
 , 
lower resident capital outflows, declining external 
debt and slowly increasing forex reserves
 ; 
ii
) 
D
emographic trends remain 
 favourable
 , even 
 though they 
 are projected to 
 weaken gradually  
in the medium
 -
 long term
 ;  
iii
) 
 Structural r
 eforms make steady progress, helping to improve financial
 -
sector supervision, ease infrastructure constraints in the 
energy and water sectors, boost aggregate productivity (
 i.e. 
 reducing the gap between high
 -
 productivity FDI firms and low
 -
productivity local sectors) and adapt to climate change.

imports are rising faster

Vietnam’s economy will continue to grow strongly in

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

RBNZ の予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZ ドル相場は一段と弱含むか 
～追加利上げの時期や総選挙を巡る不透明感が重なり、豪ドルとの対照的な動きが続くか～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、10 月 27～28 日に開催した定例理事会において、政
策金利を2.75％に据え置くことを決定した。RBNZ は７月に約３年ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、
９月にも追加利上げを実施するなど金融引き締めを強めてきた。このため、今回の据え置き
は３会合ぶりであり、金融市場の事前予想に反する決定となった。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と前期から鈍化したものの、コアインフレ率は加速す
るなど、インフレ圧力は依然として根強いことが確認された。このため、金融市場において
は RBNZ が今回利上げに動くことを織り込む動きが広がりをみせていた。こうしたなか、 今回
の決定は４対３の僅差となったほか、声明文や総裁会見でも追加利上げの必要性が示された
ものの、そのタイミングについては不透明感が残る。 
○ また、11 月７日の総選挙を控え、RBNZ の政策目標の見直しが争点となっていることも、 先行
きの政策運営を巡る不透明感につながっている。金融市場では、予想外の金利据え置きに対
する 「失望」 に加え、 金融政策が後手に回るとの警戒感もＮＺドル相場の重しとなっている。
当面はＮＺドル相場の上値が重い展開が続くと見込まれ、オセアニア通貨のなかでも豪ドル
とＮＺドルが対照的な動きをみせる可能性がある。

【RBNZは事前予想に反して、10 月の定例会合で３会合ぶりに政策金利を据え置く決定】 
ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、10 月 27～28 日に開催した定例理事会において、政策金
利であるオフィシャル・キャッシュ・レート（ＯＣＲ）を2.75％に据え置くことを決定した。ニュ
ージーランドでは、2025 年以降にインフレ率が加速し、RBNZ の定める目標レンジ（１～３％）の
上限を上回る伸びで推移している。 さらに、 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油価格の上昇がエネルギ
ーインフレを招いたことから、RBNZ は７月の定例会合で約３年ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、 ９月会合
でも追加利上げに動くなど、金融引き締めを強化してきた（注１）。このため、RBNZ による金利据

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

2/4 
え置きは３会合ぶりとなる。 
RBNZ のブレマン総裁は、 ９月の定例会合後の記者会見において、 追加利上げに含みを持たせる考
えを示していた。一方で、予め決められた方針に固執せず、追加利上げのタイミングは極めて不確
実と述べるとともに、これまでの利上げの効果などを見極める考えをみせた。声明文でも、２会合
連続の利上げ実施を受けて、 先行きは大幅利上げに動く必要性は低下したとの認識を示すなど、「タ
カ派」姿勢が後退している様子がうかがえた。こうした姿勢の変化もあり、ＲＢＮＺによる断続的
な利上げ実施にもかかわらず、その後のＮＺドル相場は調整の動きを強めた。 
同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：一院制）の総選挙が実施されるが、RBNZ のマンデート（政
策目標）が争点のひとつとなっていることも、ＮＺドル相場が調整の動きを強める一因となった。
最大野党の左派・労働党を中心とする政
党連合は、マンデートに持続可能な雇用
の最大化を再び加えることを公約に掲げ
ている。世論調査では、右派の国民党を
中心とする与党連合、左派連合の競り合
いが続いており、新興政党・オポチュニ
ティ党の動向が注目されている（注２）。
足元の雇用環境をみると、 直近４-６月の
失業率が 10 年ぶりの高水準となってお
り（図１）、 仮にマンデートが変更されれ
ば、利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が
ある。こうした事情もＮＺドル相場の重しになったと考えられる。 
一方で、RBNZ のブレマン総裁は、９月の定例会合後の記者会見において、選挙結果が政策運営に
影響を与えることはないとの見方を示し
た。このため、金融市場では今回の定例
会合を占ううえで、物価動向に注目した。
７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と
５四半期連続で目標レンジを上回ったも
のの、 前期 （同＋4.1％） から鈍化し、RBNZ
が９月の定例会合で示した見通し（前年

2.6％） から加速し、７四半期ぶりの高い

伸びとなるなど、 インフレ圧力が高まっている様子が確認された。このため、 金融市場ではRBNZ が
追加利上げに動くとの見方が広がったものの、予想に反して政策金利を据え置いた格好となった。

【僅差での据え置き決定、市場の「失望」を反映してNZドル相場は上値の重い展開が続くか】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、今回の決定について「４（据え置き）対３（25bp の利上げ）の
賛成多数で金利据え置きを決定した」とした。足元の物価動向について「想定を下回った」 、 「賃金
上昇が抑制されており、 中期的なインフレ期待は安定している」 とする一方、 「コアインフレ率は目
標レンジの上限近くに加速しており、物価上昇の動きが広範に 及びつつある」との見方を示した。
先行きについては「世界的な情勢が短期的にインフレ圧力を一段と強めている」として、その要因
に「原油高によるエネルギー価格の上昇と通貨ＮＺドル安」 を挙げた。 そして、 「10-12 月のインフ
レ率は＋4.4％に加速が見込まれる」としつつ、 「原油や石油製品価格の変動が激しく、インフレ見
通しは不透明」との見方を示した。 
実体経済については「回復が続いているものの、状況には依然ばらつきがある」との見方を示し
た。具体的には「外需関連産業では、商品市況の上昇、世界的な需要の堅調さ、外国人観光客数の
増加の恩恵を受けている」一方、 「内需関連産業は低調に推移している」とした。一方で、 「オーク
ランドを中心に住宅投資の回復の兆しがでている」とした。 
政策運営については「物価に対する一時的な要因を差し引いた上で、 経済に不必要な不安定を起
こすことを回避しつつ、中期的にインフレ率を目標レンジの中央（２％）に戻すことに注力してい
る」とあらためて強調した。 そのうえで、 「家計消費が弱く、 一部の企業ではコスト上昇の転嫁が制
限されている」 としつつ、 「インフレ率が高止まりすれば、 コスト上昇がインフレ期待や価格や賃金
に波及してインフレがより持続的なものとなるリスクが高まる」との見方を示した。 
世界経済については「堅調に推移しているものの、地政学リスクやエネルギーインフレ、金融市
場のボラティリティの高まり、ＡＩ（人工知能）関連投資の急増などを背景に、その脆弱性は高ま
っている」との見方を示した。一方で、同国経済について「回復の動きが予想以上に力強くなる可
能性はある一方、世界経済の悪化、外需の好調さが雇用や所得、家計消費に波及しなければ勢いを
失う可能性がある」との認識を示した。そのうえで、今回の決定について「政策委員は全体的なイ
ンフレ見通しでは一致したが、個々のリスクに対する見方や金融政策の対応時期に関する意見は割
れた」、先行きについても「追加利上げが必要となる可能性は高いとの見方で一致したものの、 その
タイミングは経済見通し次第」として、追加利上げに含みを持たせる考えを示した。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだブレマン総裁は、今回の協議について「景気動向を勘案すれば、より
抑制的な政策が必要になることで合意した」 、 「ＮＺドル安が続けば輸入インフレを助長する可能性
がある」との認識が共有されたとした。先行きについては「さらなる引き締めが必要となり、状況
によっては引き締め的な領域に入る可能性がある」 、 「今後の会合で追加利上げを行う可能性が高い」
との認識を示した。そして、 「ＯＣＲの道筋が９月会合の想定を上回る可能性についても協議した」
としつつ、 「中立金利の見通しには幅があり、 常に不確実性がある」 として、 具体的な水準への言及
は避けた。一方で、 今回の決定については「経済見通しに基づいた決定である」 としたうえで、 「イ

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用

向に見合う水準を下回っている」との認識を示した。 
前述したように、金融市場においては今回の利上げを織り込む動きが広がっていたため、予想外

ることがある。一部では、金融政策が後

と考えられる。このため、当面のＮＺド

の重い展開が続くなど、両通貨が対照的な動きをみせる可能性が高まっている。

Document 384: The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco_223e3a6a.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

13025075     1

Economy 
The Institute Employment Report: September 2026 
07 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Job growth softened further in September, according to Bank of America's deposit data, with growth of 1.4% year-over-year 
(YoY), from 1.5% in August. But falling unemployment payments in September indicate a relatively healthy overall picture. 
• Lower-income households' wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap. After-tax wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY 
for lower-income households in September, while rising to 3.8% and 3.7% for middle- and higher-income households, 
respectively. 
• Lower tax withholdings and a relative strengthening in the younger/lower-income end of the labor market are driving this story. 
Additionally, average hours have risen in some lower-paid industries, which is also boosting pay packets.

Job growth slowed in September, but unemployment trends are benign  
Our estimate of job growth – which uses Bank of America deposit data to track changes in the number of customer accounts 
receiving a paycheck (see Methodology) – slowed further in September, to 1.4% year-over-year (YoY) from 1.5% YoY in August. 
The data can be noisy, partly due to seasonal variation and differences in pay-period timing, but in our view, the latest reading is 
consistent with the easing in payrolls growth seen in the September Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) data (Exhibit 1). 
Still, despite slower hiring, there is little sign of a rise in joblessness in Bank of America data. Unemployment payments into 
customer accounts showed a YoY decline in September (Exhibit 2). So, overall, labor market trends appear resilient.

After-tax wage growth cooled for lower-income, but rose for other cohorts 
Lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY in September from 4.7% YoY in August, while wage growth for middle- and 
higher-income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. Overall, these changes were modest, and lower-income 
households are continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” 
pattern we observed over much of 2025 and 2026 (Exhibit 3). 
Exhibit 3: Lower-income wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap 
After-tax wage and salary growth by household income terciles, based on Bank of America aggregated consumer deposit account data (three-month moving 
average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In our view, the pick-up in lower- and middle-income households’ after-tax wage growth continues to reflect the impact of the 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBA) in reducing tax withholdings, alongside an underlying rise in job-to-job changes at the 
younger/lower-income end of the labor market (for more, read: The Institute Employment Report: August 2026). Consistent with 
that underlying pick up, BLS data shows a solid YoY rise in average hours worked in several relatively lower-paid industries, such 
as manufacturing, retail and other services. Hourly workers in these industries are likely seeing rising paychecks as a result 
(Exhibit 4).

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Estimate of payroll growth from Bank of America internal data is based on the change in customer accounts receiving a paycheck in the month. 
An adjustment is made for the difference between overall population growth and customer account growth. Occasionally historic payments can 
be reclassified, leading to revisions in the series.  
An estimate of bonus growth from Bank of America deposit data is calculated by looking at customers who have received an inbound ACH 
payroll transaction in the last two years. From this sample an estimate of bonuses is derived by looking for payroll transactions which are over 
50% higher than the median regular payroll payments received by the customer. Of these payments only those that were received around the 
same time in each of the last two years are selected.  
The job-to-job change rate (j2j rate) is defined as the proportion of customers with an identified change in their employer as a proportion of 
the total number of customers with employment income. We estimate the median pay rise associated with a j2j change using the pay in the 
first three months of the new job compared to the same three months a year ago. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
US-China talks, a possible Trump-Xi-Putin trilateral meeting, and any signs of a China-Japan diplomatic
thaw will be critical watchpoints. The priorities of the summit between representatives of 21 Asia-Pacific
economies are set to include efforts to boost innovation, digital connectivity, supply-chain resilience and

support for an open multilateral trading system. During the summit, presidents Trump and Xi are expected to
meet for the third time this year. This meeting will provide signals on the state of China-US talks, including the
operationalisation of new bilateral trade and investment boards, and on the likelihood of another extension of
the Busan trade truce beyond January 2027. President Trump has also indicated he is open to a trilateral US-
China-Russia meeting during the summit, possibly covering nuclear arms control and the Russia-Ukraine war.

Investors will also closely watch whether Japanese Prime Minister Takaichi and President Xi meet on the
sidelines, following reports that Tokyo is hoping to arrange a meeting. However, the bar for a formal
breakthrough will likely remain high. As we noted in a report earlier this year, a full normalisation of relations
and an easing of Chinese economic restrictions – which include rare-earth export controls – may require a
lengthy period of diplomatic re-engagement and backchannel diplomacy.

A divided government could mean less change at home but more abroad. Third-party polling and betting
markets forecast Democrat control of the House, and an increasing probability of flipping the Senate. 80% of
respondents in our monthly poll now say living costs will influence their vote – the most since April. For
markets, a divided government could mean less domestic legislative change, but not necessarily a more
predictable US foreign policy. Losing Congress could leave theadministration’s geopolitical and geoeconomic

toolkit largely intact.
In our view, weapons stockpiles, inflationary pressures and long-end yields may matter more than Congress
in constraining theadministration’s choices in 2027 and 2028. A Democratic House could dial up oversight of
the administration, launch investigations, force votes on War Powers Resolutions, and use appropriations bills
to press for restrictions on departmental funding. This may generate a steady stream of attention-grabbing

headlines, but whether it decisively affects the event-driven risk that matters for markets (e.g. blocking a tariff
or preventing a limited military strike) remains in doubt. If domestic policymaking becomes more challenging,
foreign policy could become a top priority for the Trump administration.

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
NewZealand’s general election is shaping up as a close and fragmented contest. Both the ruling National
and opposition Labour parties are polling at historically low levels, and smaller parties will likely determine
the next government. In a closely contested vote, investors could see coalition uncertainty and a wider

range of possible outcomes for tax, spending, infrastructure, energy and migration policy.
The National party is campaigning on a platform that would broadly preserve the current emphasis on
fiscal restraint, lower regulation and private-sector investment. A Labour-led government could place
greater weight on public services, housing and climate policy, alongside a proposed capital-gains tax on
investment property. However, because any government will rely on minor parties for survival, the final

policy mix will depend heavily on post-election negotiations.
The meeting will be a test of the EU’s ability to translate the competitiveness agenda into practical
reforms. The focus will be on Europe’s ability to restore competitiveness, mobilize investment and
maintain fiscal discipline. The Eurogroup brings together Euro-area finance ministers and is expected to
consider macroeconomic and fiscal developments, energy market pressures and progress towards the

Savings and Investments Union. ECOFIN brings together all EU finance ministers, and will focus on
financial integration, banking sector competitiveness, and economic coordination. To watch will be
tensions between fiscal consolidation and the need to fund defence, energy security and strategic
investment, alongside progress on integratingEurope’s fragmented capital markets and banking sectors. A
budget-focused Council meeting follows on 10 November.

Implementation of existing commitments, rather than new policy pledges, is expected to be the primary
focus of the next climate summit in Antalya. Building on the shift towards implementation at COP30,
COP31 will focus on translating existing climate commitments into practical action, including through
investment, financing and partnerships. For investors, the key watchpoint will be whether the summit can
deliver tangible commitments that translate existing plans into binding funding pledges for critical

infrastructure resilience, scaled-up renewable energy output, and climate finance.

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
Melaka’s state election will offer a further gauge of Malaysia’s evolving electoral landscape after
strong recent state-level performances by Barisan Nasional (BN). The contest will also be watched for
its read-through to Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s Pakatan Harapan (PH), following setbacks in other

state elections this year.
BN enters the election from a position of relative strength, while its electoral understanding with PAS
and its allies could provide an early indication of how political alignments are evolving ahead of the next
general election. However, Melaka’s local dynamics mean the result should not be treated as a direct
national forecast.
The anticipated signing of a landmark digital economy agreement is set to be one of the most

significant summit deliverables in years. The 49th ASEAN Summit in Manila will focus on economic
integration, energy security, supply-chain resilience, maritime security, the crisis in Myanmar, and
relations with external partners.
For markets, the formal signing of the ASEAN Digital Economy Framework Agreement (DEFA) is the key
event to watch. Designed as a legally binding treaty, DEFA aims to harmonize regional digital trade rules
and cross-border data flows. Successful implementation is expected to double the projected size of the

region’s digital economy by 2030, boosting it from a projected baseline of US$1 trillion to US$2 trillion
(up from approximately US$300 billion today).
The second EU-UK Summit will be an important test of whether the post-Brexit relationship can move
towards tangible outcomes. Prime Minister Andy Burnham will seek to finalize agreements on food and
agricultural standards, youth mobility, and linking emissions trading systems. Burnham has framed
relations as “not good enough.” To watch will be whether we see concrete deals versus declarations, and

whether leaders are prepared to make the concessions necessary to deepen ties.

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
The upcoming local elections could serve as a political barometer ahead of the 2028 general election–
but only up to a point. Local elections in Taiwan are primarily driven by municipal ‘bread and butter’
economic issues; geopolitics and cross-Strait relations have historically not been at the forefront of

voters’ concerns. While this divergence limits theelections’ predictive value for the January 2028 general
vote, the results can provide a sense of the domestic balance of power.
Elections in Taiwan’s six special municipalities – Taipei, New Taipei, Taichung, Tainan, Taoyuan, and
Kaohsiung – will serve as particularly important watchpoints. Some recent polling suggests a potentially
close contest in Taipei between incumbent mayor Chiang Wan-an (Kuomintang) and legislator Shen Po-

yang (Democratic Progressive Party).
Spain’s snap general election could mark a major turning point for Spanish politics and the EU,
potentially ending eight years of Socialist-led government under Pedro Sánchez and bringing the
conservative People’s Party (PP) to power with support from the far-right Vox. Polling suggests PP is
ahead and a PP-Vox alignment is the only one projected to reach a majority. A rightward shift in one of
the EU’s largest economies could occur just as Europe enters a wider electoral cycle, potentially

complicating consensus on migration, climate policy, Ukraine and European integration.
The Forum comes at a pivotal moment in EU-Latin American relations. It is the EU’s first big commercial
push into Argentina since the EU-Mercosur trade deal began to be provisionally applied on 1 May. The
agreement opens new opportunities for trade, investment and market access. The EU is Argentina’s
largest source of foreign direct investment. Around 800 government, business and financial leaders will

focus on energy, critical minerals, agriculture and infrastructure. The EU’s Global Gateway investment
agenda provides a framework for mobilizing public and private capital. While a key outcome will be the
launch of a structured EU-Argentina investment dialogue, to watch will be the ability of Argentine
officials to allay concerns around policy stability and regulatory predictability.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
A potential Trump-Kim meeting in November could restart official bilateral dialogue, though North
Korea remains highly unlikely to abandon its nuclear program. South Korea’s National Intelligence
Service said in late September that it saw a roughly 50% chance of US-North Korea talks around the
APEC Summit in Shenzhen, although more recent reporting suggests the prospects may have

diminished. If talks do resume, they would only mark the first step in a long and highly uncertain
diplomatic process.
As we noted earlier this year, North Korea is highly unlikely to agree to denuclearization. Unless renewed
negotiations sidestep denuclearization in favor of a risk-reduction or coexistence framework, dialogue is
likely to stall, not least given Pyongyang’s constitutional pledge to continue expanding its nuclear
arsenal. This would make even a negotiated freeze on further expansion difficult to achieve.

semiconductor share prices have recently lagged the trajectory of near-term EPS

### Page 3

The magnitude of S&P 500 “over-earning”

S&P 500 earnings grew by an extraordinary 51% year/year in Q2 2026 and have 
grown by 26% during the past four quarters. During the last 30 years, the recent pace 
of earnings growth has been exceeded only during the post-recession rebounds in 2010 
and 2021.

The recent strength of S&P 500 earnings growth has raised investor concerns that 
current earnings power is unsustainable and that the market is in an “earnings 
bubble.” S&P 500 earnings have recently outpaced both their historical trend and their 
historical relationship with US GDP growth. S&P 500 4-quarter EPS growth has averaged 
7% during the past 30 years, and the relationship between S&P 500 earnings and US 
economic growth in recent decades would have pointed to a similar rate of earnings 
growth during the last few quarters.

Goldman Sachs US Equity Views

### Page 4

The degree to which the US equity market is currently “expensive” depends in large 
part on the sustainability of recent earnings strength. The exceptional recent 
strength of earnings has driven an unusual wedge between equity valuation multiples 
based on near-term earnings and valuations on trend earnings. Because equity prices 
have failed to keep pace with surging earnings, near-term valuations show no hint of a 
bubble: The S&P 500 12-month P/E ratio has declined from 23x a year ago to 19x today 
and now matches its 10-year average. However, even an “average” multiple may be 
expensive if current earnings are unsustainable. The cyclically-adjusted P/E based on 
trailing 10-year earnings has climbed to one of its highest levels on record, falling shy of 
the highs of 1999-2000 but surpassing the level reached in 2021. Similarly, our 
colleagues have argued that the increase in market value of AI companies during the last 
few years requires an optimistic estimate of the future magnitude of AI-related profits 
and who will capture them.

### Page 5

The AI boom and S&P 500 earnings growth

Affirming investor concerns that US companies are “over-earning,” there are three 
factors that are boosting earnings today but will contribute to deceleration going 
forward: 1) AI capex spending, 2) semiconductor margin expansion, and 3) earnings 
from private investment gains.

AI capex 
The AI investment boom is driving nearly half of S&P 500 EPS growth this year, but 
its contribution will fade going forward. The mega-cap US hyperscalers are on track to 
spend $800 billion on capex this year, an increase of 94% vs. 2025. That spending is 
flowing through to the earnings of the AI infrastructure complex including 
semiconductor, tech hardware, industrials, and utilities companies, which are collectively 
accounting for roughly half of consensus S&P 500 earnings growth this year. The AI 
boom is also having a secondary impact on earnings outside of the infrastructure 
complex, for example by boosting capital markets activity and consumer wealth. Both 
consensus and GS analyst forecasts show hyperscaler capex growing at a slower rate in 
coming years, which should result in decelerating earnings growth for much of the AI 
infrastructure complex and a fading tailwind to S&P 500 earnings.

Hyperscaler depreciation expenses will continue to increase as capex growth 
decelerates, further dampening the boost of AI investment spending to S&P 500 
earnings growth. We estimate a drag from hyperscaler depreciation expenses on S&P 
500 earnings growth of 5pp in 2027, offsetting nearly half of the 11pp boost to earnings 
from capex spending. By 2028, the drag from depreciation should offset the S&P 500 
earnings uplift from continued capex spending.

### Page 7

If capex were fully expensed, consensus 2027 S&P 500 EPS would be 22% lower than 
under current accounting rules. The current gap between actual reported and 
hypothetical fully-expensed earnings is one of the widest in recent decades, exceeded 
only as earnings fell more quickly than capex during the recessions of 2001 and 2008.

Hyperscaler capex has consistently surprised relative to consensus estimates during 
the last few years, and the potential for additional surprises going forward creates a 
wide range of potential S&P 500 earnings outcomes. Our equity analysts expect 
hyperscaler capex will total $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028, in which case 
the current tailwind from AI capex to S&P 500 EPS will fade by 2028. Exhibit 10 below 
outlines upside and downside scenarios around that base case. Next year, a $250 billion 
surprise in hyperscaler capex — in either direction — would shift S&P 500 EPS growth by 
about 6 pp in the same direction.  
We estimate that hyperscaler capex would need to decline by roughly 30%, to $570 
billion, to offset earnings growth for the rest of the S&P 500 in 2027. This decline 
would reduce earnings for the AI infrastructure complex by about 40%, leaving their 
profits about 25% above 2025 levels.

### Page 8

Semiconductor margin expansion 
A sharp increase in profit margins for many semiconductor companies has 
contributed to the above-trend recent pace of S&P 500 earnings growth. The 
combination of strong demand and limited supply has boosted semiconductor prices 
and gross margins, with the most pronounced margin expansion among memory stocks. 
S&P 500 memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double 
their historical average. Across the semiconductor industry, gross margins sit at their 
highest level in decades, and consensus expects margins to remain at similar levels 
through 2028.

Margin expansion has accounted for a large share of recent semiconductor earnings 
growth, but that boost should fade going forward. We estimate that about 1/4 of S&P 
500 Semiconductor earnings growth in 2026 is being driven by gross margin expansion. 
Our equity analysts believe the balance of memory supply and demand is likely to 
remain tight through 2027, with incremental supply additions unlikely to impact industry 
margins until 2028 at the earliest. However, they expect the rate of incremental margin 
expansion to slow meaningfully next year, reducing the contribution of semiconductors

S&P 500 EPS growth by about 1 pp.
